T>\nT kahan_sum(const std::vector<T>& data) {\n    T sum = 0.0;\n    T c = 0.0;\n    for (const auto& val : data) {\n        T y = val - c;\n        T t = sum + y;\n        c = (t - sum) - y;\n        sum = t;\n    }\n    return sum;\n}\n\n// Print results for floating-point arrays\ntemplate <typename T>\nvoid print_results(const std::vector<T>& data, const char* name = \"Result\") {\n    if (data.empty()) {\n        printf(\"=== RESULTS ===\\n\");\n        printf(\"Name: %s\\n\", name);\n        printf(\"Elements: 0\\n\");\n        printf(\"=== END RESULTS ===\\n\");\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const size_t N = data.size();\n    \n    // Compute statistics\n    T sum = kahan_sum(data);\n    T min_val = data[0];\n    T max_val = data[0];\n    for (const auto& val : data) {\n        min_val = std::min(min_val, val);\n        max_val = std::max(max_val, val);\n    }\n    \n    // Compute hash\n    hash h;\n    for (const auto& val : data) {\n        h.add(val);\n    }\n    \n    // Sample indices: 0, N/4, N/2, 3N/4, N-1\n    size_t indices[5] = {0, N/4, N/2, 3*N/4, N-1};\n    \n    printf(\"=== RESULTS ===\\n\");\n    printf(\"Name: %s\\n\", name);\n    printf(\"Elements: Edge cases also work: more ranks than options (`-np 8 -n 3`) and `-n 0`.\n\n**How it works**\n- **Each rank does its own share:** the index range is split into nearly equal contiguous blocks. Each rank builds only its own options, which is exact because every option depends only on its global index. Nothing is sent out from rank 0 and no rank holds the full array.\n- **Results are collected only when needed:** they go back to rank 0 with point-to-point messages, and only when `-r` or `-v` is given. Messages are split into pieces so very large `-n` doesn't overflow MPI's `int` counts.\n- **Timing:** there's a barrier before and after the pricing loop, so the reported time is the slowest rank's, measured over the same region as the original.\n- **Output and exit codes:** only rank 0 prints.
